Wait, let's check if castling is legal.
1. The king and rook must not have moved. (Assume they haven't).
2. There must be no pieces between the king and rook. (e1 and h1: f1 and g1 are empty).
3. The king must not be in check. (The king is at e1. Black's pieces: Q-d8, R-a8, R-f8, N-d7. None can attack e1).
4. The king must not pass through a square that is under attack. (f1 and g1. Let's check).
- Does any Black piece attack f1?
  - Q-d8: No.
  - R-a8: No.
  - R-f8: Yes! The rook is at f8. It attacks f1!
  - N-d7: No.
Wait! The rook at f8 attacks the entire f-file!
So the square f1 is under attack.
If f1 is under attack, the king cannot pass through it.
Therefore, castling kingside (e1g1) is ILLEGAL.

Let me re-verify this.
Rook on f8.
King on e1.
To castle kingside, the king moves e1 -> g1.
The squares it passes through are e1, f1, g1.
Since the rook on f8 attacks f1, the king is passing through a square under attack.
According to the rules of chess, you cannot castle if the king passes through a square that is under attack.
